     1→# CLAUDE.md
     2→
     3→This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
     4→
     5→## Project Overview
     6→
     7→Sandcastle is a self-hosted shared Docker sandbox platform. Users get isolated Sysbox containers with SSH access and a full Docker daemon inside. The stack is Rails 8.1 (Ruby 4.0) for the web/API backend and a Go CLI for user interaction.
     8→
     9→> **Important:** The container runtime is **Docker + Sysbox** (`sysbox-runc`). Incus/LXD is **not** used and should never be introduced. All container operations go through the `docker-api` gem via the Docker socket.
    10→
    11→## Commands
    12→
    13→### Rails App
    14→
    15→```bash
    16→bin/dev                              # Start dev server (web + Tailwind watcher via foreman)
    17→bin/rails test                       # Run all tests (Minitest)
    18→bin/rails test test/models/user_test.rb          # Run a single test file
    19→bin/rails test test/models/user_test.rb:10       # Run a single test by line
    20→bin/rails db:migrate                 # Run pending migrations
    21→bin/rails db:prepare                 # Migrate + seed
    22→bin/rubocop                          # Lint (rubocop-rails-omakase style)
    23→bin/brakeman                         # Security scan
    24→```
    25→
    26→### Go CLI (vendor/sandcastle-cli/)
    27→
    28→```bash
    29→cd vendor/sandcastle-cli && make build    # Build binary → ./sandcastle
    30→cd vendor/sandcastle-cli && go build ./...  # Quick compile check
    31→```
    32→
    33→Go module path is `github.com/sandcastle/cli`. When adding a new feature: add types to `api/types.go`, client methods to `api/client.go`, then the Cobra command in `cmd/`.
    34→
    35→### Full CI
    36→
    37→```bash
    38→bin/ci    # Runs rubocop → brakeman → bundler-audit → importmap audit → tests → system tests
    39→```
    40→
    41→### Releasing
    42→
    43→```bash
    44→mise run release          # Bump patch (v0.1.14 → v0.1.15), tag, push
    45→mise run release:minor    # Bump minor (v0.1.14 → v0.2.0), tag, push
    46→mise run release:major    # Bump major (v0.1.14 → v1.0.0), tag, push
    47→```
    48→
    49→Pushing a tag triggers the GitHub Actions `release.yml` workflow which:
    50→1. Builds CLI binaries via GoReleaser (cross-compiled for linux/darwin × amd64/arm64)
    51→2. Creates a GitHub release with signed/notarized archives
    52→3. Auto-updates the Homebrew formula in `thieso2/homebrew-tap` (requires `HOMEBREW_TAP_GITHUB_TOKEN` secret)
    53→
    54→CLI version is injected at build time via ldflags (`-X github.com/sandcastle/cli/cmd.Version`).
    55→
    56→## Architecture
    57→
    58→### Two Auth Systems
    59→
    60→- **Web UI**: Session-based via `Authentication` concern → cookie `session_id`
    61→- **API**: Bearer token via `ApiAuthentication` concern → `Authorization: Bearer sc_PREFIX_SECRET` header, bcrypt digest stored in `api_tokens` table. Token format: `sc_{8-char prefix}_{48-char secret}` (prefix stored plain, secret bcrypt-hashed)
    62→
    63→Both concerns provide `require_admin!` for admin-only actions.
    64→
    65→### Service Objects
    66→
    67→Business logic lives in plain Ruby service classes, not models or controllers:
    68→- `SandboxManager` — Container lifecycle (create/destroy/start/stop/snapshot/restore). Uses the `docker-api` gem to talk to the Docker daemon via socket.
    69→- `TailscaleManager` — Per-user Tailscale sidecar lifecycle (see Tailscale section below)
    70→- `SystemStatus` — Docker daemon stats for admin dashboard
    71→
    72→All service errors inherit from `ServiceName::Error` and are rescued in `Api::BaseController` as 422 JSON responses. `ActiveRecord::RecordNotFound` maps to 404, `RecordInvalid` to 422.
    73→
    74→### Container Model
    75→
    76→Each sandbox = one Sysbox container (`sysbox-runc` runtime) with:
    77→- SSH on a unique host port (range 2201–2299)
    78→- User home bind-mounted from `/data/users/{name}/home`
    79→- Optional persistent volume at `/data/sandboxes/{name}/vol:/workspace`
    80→- Optional Tailscale connectivity via per-user bridge network + sidecar
    81→
    82→The sandbox image (`images/sandbox/`) is Ubuntu 24.04 with Docker-in-Docker, SSH server, and dev tools. Key details:
    83→- runc pinned to v1.1.15 (1.2+ fails in Sysbox)
    84→- Docker daemon MTU matches container's eth0 to avoid fragmentation
    85→- The `entrypoint.sh` creates the user, injects SSH keys, starts dockerd, then runs sshd
    86→
    87→### Tailscale System
    88→
    89→Per-user Tailscale sidecars connect sandbox containers to a user's own tailnet. Each user gets their own sidecar joining **their** tailnet (not a shared one).
    90→
    91→**Two authentication flows in `TailscaleManager`:**
    92→- **Auth key flow** (`enable`): One-shot with `TS_AUTHKEY` env var, uses default `containerboot` entrypoint
    93→- **Interactive login flow** (`start_login` → `check_login`): Overrides entrypoint to `tailscaled` directly, runs `tailscale up --reset --timeout=10s` to get a browser login URL, polls `tailscale status --json` for `BackendState == "Running"`
    94→
    95→**User `tailscale_state` field:** `disabled` → `pending` (during interactive login) → `enabled`
    96→
    97→**Sidecar architecture:**
    98→- Bridge network: `sc-ts-net-{username}` with subnet `172.{100+user_id%100}.0.0/16`
    99→- Container: `sc-ts-{username}` running `tailscale/tailscale:latest`
   100→- State persisted at `/data/users/{name}/tailscale`
   101→- Advertises subnet routes so sandboxes on the bridge are reachable from the tailnet
   102→- `tailscale_auto_connect` user setting auto-joins new sandboxes to the bridge network
   103→
   104→**Important:** `containerboot` (default Tailscale image entrypoint) conflicts with manual `tailscale up` when no auth key is provided. The interactive flow must override the entrypoint to run `tailscaled` directly.
   105→
   106→### Background Jobs
   107→
   108→Solid Queue with recurring schedule in `config/recurring.yml`:
   109→- `ContainerSyncJob` — every 5 min, reconciles DB sandbox status with actual Docker container state and Tailscale sidecar health
   110→
   111→### Database
   112→
   113→PostgreSQL with 4 separate databases in production (`config/database.yml`):
   114→- `sandcastle_production` — primary (users, sandboxes, api_tokens, sessions)
   115→- `sandcastle_production_cache` — Solid Cache
   116→- `sandcastle_production_queue` — Solid Queue
   117→- `sandcastle_production_cable` — Solid Cable
   118→
   119→The primary database is created by PostgreSQL's `POSTGRES_DB` env var. The other 3 are created by a PostgreSQL init script (`docker/postgres/init-databases.sh`, mounted into `/docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/`). The installer writes this script to `$REDACTED.sh`. When adding or removing Solid* databases, update both the init script and `database.yml`.
   120→
   121→Key constraints:
   122→- Sandbox names and SSH ports are unique only among non-destroyed sandboxes (partial unique indexes: `WHERE status != 'destroyed'`)
   123→- SSH port auto-assigned from 2201–2299 on create
   124→
   125→### Frontend
   126→
   127→ERB templates with Tailwind CSS (v4). Turbo Frames for async container stats. No heavy JS framework — Stimulus controllers only where needed. The Tailscale pending state uses vanilla JS polling (fetch every 3s) to detect auth completion.
   128→
   129→## Deployment
   130→
   131→- **Host**: `100.106.185.92` (Tailscale IP), SSH user `thies`
   132→- **Registry**: `ghcr.io/thieso2/sandcastle` (GitHub Container Registry)
   133→- **Deploy**: `kamal deploy` (config in `config/deploy.yml`), or `docker-compose up -d`
   134→- **Host bootstrap**: `bootstrap/sandcastle-bootstrap.sh` (Docker, Sysbox, Caddy, UFW)
   135→- **Env vars**: `SECRET_KEY_BASE`, `RAILS_MASTER_KEY`, `SANDCASTLE_HOST`, `SANDCASTLE_DATA_DIR` (default `/data`)
   136→- **Proxy**: kamal-proxy with `response_timeout: 60` (needed for Tailscale login flow which blocks ~13s)
   137→- The Kamal app container needs `group-add: 988` for Docker socket access
   138→
   139→## Conventions
   140→
   141→- When features are changed or added, update the guide page at `app/views/pages/guide.html.erb` to reflect the new CLI commands and usage.
   142→
```

     1→# Deployment
     2→
     3→## Registry
     4→
     5→- **App image:** `ghcr.io/thieso2/sandcastle`
     6→- **Sandbox image:** `ghcr.io/thieso2/sandcastle-sandbox`
     7→- **Auth:** GitHub PAT with `write:packages` scope, stored as `GITHUB_TOKEN` in `.env`
     8→
     9→## Deploy Workflows
    10→
    11→### Full Release (CI-built image)
    12→
    13→```mermaid
    14→flowchart LR
    15→    A["git tag\nv0.1.3"] --> B["git push\norigin v0.1.3"]
    16→    B --> C["GH Actions builds\napp + sandbox"]
    17→    C --> D["kamal deploy\n--skip-push\n--version=0.1.3"]
    18→```
    19→
    20→```bash
    21→git tag v0.1.3
    22→git push origin v0.1.3
    23→# wait for CI to finish (check https://github.com/thieso2/Sandcastle/actions)
    24→kamal deploy --skip-push --version=0.1.3
    25→```
    26→
    27→CI builds both images in parallel and pushes with semver tags (`0.1.3`, `0.1`, `0`, `latest`).
    28→
    29→### Quick Hotfix (local build)
    30→
    31→```bash
    32→kamal deploy
    33→```
    34→
    35→Builds on your machine and pushes directly to ghcr.io. No tag or CI needed.
    36→
    37→## Setup (one-time, on deploy host)
    38→
    39→1. Create a GitHub PAT (classic) with `write:packages` scope
    40→2. Add to `.env` on the deploy host and locally:
    41→   ```bash
    42→   GITHUB_TOKEN=ghp_...
    43→   ```
    44→3. Verify login:
    45→   ```bash
    46→   docker login ghcr.io -u thieso2 --password-stdin <<< $GITHUB_TOKEN
    47→   ```
    48→
```

     1→<% content_for(:title) { "Getting Started — Sandcastle" } %>
     2→
     3→<div class="max-w-3xl mx-auto px-4 py-8">
     4→  <h1 class="text-3xl font-bold text-gray-900 mb-2">Getting Started</h1>
     5→  <p class="text-gray-500 mb-8">Everything you need to go from zero to a running sandbox.</p>
     6→
     7→  <% host = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_HOST", "sandcastle.example.com") %>
     8→
     9→  <%# ── Install ── %>
    10→  <section class="mb-10">
    11→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">1. Install the CLI</h2>
    12→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
    13→      Download the latest release from
    14→      <%= link_to "GitHub Releases", "https://github.com/thieso2/Sandcastle/releases/latest", target: "_blank", class: "text-blue-600 hover:text-blue-800 underline" %>,
    15→      then extract it:
    16→    </p>
    17→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code>tar xzf sandcastle_*.tar.gz
    18→sudo mv sandcastle /usr/local/bin/</code></pre>
    19→  </section>
    20→
    21→  <%# ── Login ── %>
    22→  <section class="mb-10">
    23→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">2. Log in</h2>
    24→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">Point the CLI at this server and authenticate via your browser:</p>
    25→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code>sandcastle login https://<%= host %></code></pre>
    26→  </section>
    27→
    28→  <%# ── Tailscale ── %>
    29→  <section class="mb-10">
    30→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">3. Enable Tailscale <span class="text-sm font-normal text-gray-500">(recommended)</span></h2>
    31→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
    32→      Each sandbox gets its own Tailscale IP so you can reach services directly — no port forwarding needed.
    33→    </p>
    34→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code># Interactive login — opens a browser URL to authenticate
    35→sandcastle tailscale enable
    36→
    37→# Or use an auth key for headless setups
    38→sandcastle tailscale enable --auth-key tskey-auth-...</code></pre>
    39→    <p class="text-gray-500 text-sm mt-2">After authenticating, approve the advertised subnet routes in the Tailscale admin console.</p>
    40→  </section>
    41→
    42→  <%# ── Create ── %>
    43→  <section class="mb-10">
    44→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">4. Create a sandbox</h2>
    45→
    46→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3"><strong>Quick throwaway sandbox</strong> — deleted when you disconnect:</p>
    47→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code>sandcastle create scratch --rm</code></pre>
    48→
    49→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3 mt-4"><strong>Persistent sandbox</strong> — keeps your home directory and data across restarts:</p>
    50→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code>sandcastle create my-dev --home --data</code></pre>
    51→
    52→    <div class="bg-blue-50 border border-blue-200 rounded-lg p-4 mt-4 text-sm text-blue-800">
    53→      <strong>Tip:</strong> Set environment variables to make flags the default:<br>
    54→      <code class="bg-blue-100 px-1 rounded">export SANDCASTLE_HOME=1 SANDCASTLE_DATA=. SANDCASTLE_RM=1</code>
    55→    </div>
    56→  </section>
    57→
    58→  <%# ── Connect ── %>
    59→  <section class="mb-10">
    60→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">5. Connect</h2>
    61→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">Two ways to get a shell:</p>
    62→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code># tmux session (reconnectable, auto-starts stopped sandboxes)
    63→sandcastle connect my-dev
    64→
    65→# Plain SSH
    66→sandcastle ssh my-dev</code></pre>
    67→  </section>
    68→
    69→  <%# ── Web Terminal ── %>
    70→  <section class="mb-10">
    71→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">6. Web terminal</h2>
    72→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
    73→      Click the <strong>Terminal</strong> button on any running sandbox to open a browser-based shell.
    74→      No SSH client needed — just a web browser.
    75→    </p>
    76→    <div class="bg-blue-50 border border-blue-200 rounded-lg p-4 text-sm text-blue-800">
    77→      <strong>How it works:</strong> Sandcastle spins up an ephemeral WeTTY container that auto-connects to your sandbox via SSH.
    78→      The terminal session is protected by your existing login — no extra passwords required.
    79→    </div>
    80→  </section>
    81→
    82→  <%# ── Manage ── %>
    83→  <section class="mb-10">
    84→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">7. Manage sandboxes</h2>
    85→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code>sandcastle list              # Show all your sandboxes
    86→sandcastle stop my-dev       # Stop a running sandbox
    87→sandcastle start my-dev      # Start a stopped sandbox
    88→sandcastle delete my-dev     # Permanently remove a sandbox</code></pre>
    89→  </section>
    90→
    91→  <%# ── Routes ── %>
    92→  <section class="mb-10">
    93→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">8. Custom domain routes</h2>
    94→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">
    95→      Expose services running inside your sandbox on custom domains with automatic TLS.
    96→      Each sandbox can have multiple routes pointing to different ports:
    97→    </p>
    98→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code># Add a route — app.example.com → port 8080 (default) in sandbox "myapp"
    99→sandcastle route add myapp app.example.com
   100→
   101→# Add another route on a custom port
   102→sandcastle route add myapp api.example.com 3000
   103→
   104→# List all routes for a sandbox
   105→sandcastle route list myapp
   106→
   107→# Remove a specific route
   108→sandcastle route delete myapp app.example.com</code></pre>
   109→    <p class="text-gray-500 text-sm mt-2">Point a DNS A/CNAME record at <code class="bg-gray-100 px-1 rounded"><%= host %></code> before adding the route. TLS certificates are provisioned automatically via Let's Encrypt.</p>
   110→  </section>
   111→
   112→  <%# ── Snapshots ── %>
   113→  <section class="mb-10">
   114→    <h2 class="text-xl font-semibold text-gray-900 mb-3">9. Snapshots</h2>
   115→    <p class="text-gray-700 mb-3">Save and restore the full state of a sandbox:</p>
   116→    <pre class="bg-gray-900 text-gray-100 rounded-lg p-4 overflow-x-auto text-sm leading-relaxed"><code># Create a snapshot
   117→sandcastle snapshot create my-dev my-checkpoint
   118→
   119→# List snapshots
   120→sandcastle snapshot list
   121→
   122→# Restore into a new sandbox
   123→sandcastle create my-dev-v2 --snapshot my-checkpoint</code></pre>
   124→  </section>
   125→
   126→  <div class="border-t border-gray-200 pt-6 text-center">
   127→    <p class="text-sm text-gray-500">
   128→      Run <code class="bg-gray-100 px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded">sandcastle --help</code> for the full command reference.
   129→    </p>
   130→  </div>
   131→</div>
   132→

  "new_string": "### Web Terminal (WeTTY)\n\nBrowser-based terminal access via [WeTTY](https://github.com/butlerx/wetty) sidecar containers. Each terminal open spawns an ephemeral WeTTY container that SSH's into the sandbox.\n\n**Architecture:**\n- WeTTY container: `sc-wetty-{user}-{sandbox}` on the `sandcastle-web` Docker network\n- SSH keypair: generated per-open in `/data/wetty/{full_name}/`, private key copied into WeTTY container via `docker exec` + base64 (bind mounts don't work because Rails runs inside a container with a named Docker volume for `/data`)\n- Traefik dynamic config: `terminal-{id}.yml` in `/data/traefik/dynamic/` with forwardAuth middleware\n- WeTTY uses `COMMAND` env var with full SSH command including tmux (`tmux new-session -A -s main`)\n- `SANDCASTLE_TERMINAL_URL` env var controls the redirect target (needed when Rails is behind a different port than Traefik)\n\n**ForwardAuth flow (`TerminalController#auth`):**\n- Traefik sends each request to `http://sandcastle-web:80/terminal/auth` with `X-Forwarded-Uri`\n- Rails validates the session cookie and sandbox ownership (owner or admin)\n- Unauthenticated users get redirected to the login page with `return_to_after_authenticating` set to the terminal URL\n- Returns 200 to allow, redirect to login, or 401 for unauthorized\n\n**Important gotchas:**\n- Sandbox entrypoint must **append** SSH keys (not overwrite) because all sandboxes for the same user share `/home/{user}` via bind mount\n- `remove_pubkey` uses end-of-line regex anchor (`$`) to avoid substring collisions between sandbox names\n- Traefik config is written early in `TerminalManager#open` (before container creation) with a 1-second sleep to allow Traefik to detect the new route before the browser redirect arrives\n\n### Tailscale System"
}
```

     1→# Sandcastle Project Memory
     2→
     3→## Project Overview
     4→- Self-hosted shared sandbox platform (Rails 8.1 + Go CLI)
     5→- Each sandbox = Docker + Sysbox (`sysbox-runc`) container with SSH + Docker-in-Docker
     6→- Located at `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle`
     7→
     8→## Tech Stack
     9→- **Backend:** Rails 8.1.1 (Ruby 4.0), PostgreSQL, Solid Queue/Cache/Cable
    10→- **CLI:** Go, Cobra, `~/.sandcastle/config.yaml`
    11→- **Containers:** Docker + Sysbox for sandboxes, Docker for Rails app (Kamal deploy)
    12→- **Auth:** Rails 8 generated auth + API tokens (bcrypt, `sc_` prefix)
    13→
    14→## CRITICAL: No Incus
    15→- Incus/LXD is NOT used — project is 100% Docker + Sysbox
    16→- `docker-api` gem talks to Docker daemon via socket
    17→- Do NOT introduce Incus, cloud-init, or LXD concepts
    18→- Previous memory entries about Incus/IncusClient/cloud-init were WRONG
    19→
    20→## Key Architecture Decisions
    21→- Sysbox containers with Docker-in-Docker
    22→- SSH port range: 2201-2299 via Docker port bindings
    23→- Persistent volumes at `/data/sandboxes/{name}/vol` via bind mounts
    24→- User homes at `/data/users/{name}/home` via bind mounts
    25→- API auth via `Authorization: Bearer sc_PREFIX_SECRET`
    26→- `entrypoint.sh` creates user, injects SSH keys, starts dockerd, runs sshd
    27→- Snapshots via `docker commit`
    28→
    29→## Rails Notes
    30→- `rails new --css=tailwind` (not tailwindcss) on Rails 8.1
    31→- Auth generator: `bin/rails generate authentication`
    32→- `app/lib/` is in autoload paths via Rails 8 conventions
    33→
    34→## Tailscale
    35→- Per-user sidecar architecture (TailscaleManager service)
    36→- Sidecar container: `sc-ts-{username}` on bridge network `sc-ts-net-{username}`
    37→- Two flows: auth key (TS_AUTHKEY env) and interactive login (tailscaled + tailscale up)
    38→- User `tailscale_state`: disabled → pending → enabled
    39→
    40→## Installer
    41→- `installer.sh` (was `install.sh`) — subcommands: `install` (default), `reset`, `uninstall`
    42→- `install-defaults` — documents ALL configurable vars with defaults
    43→- **Rule:** Always keep `install-defaults` in sync when adding/changing vars in `installer.sh`
    44→- Config layout: `$SANDCASTLE_HOME/etc/env` (sandcastle), `$SANDCASTLE_HOME/etc/env.dockyard` (dockyard)
    45→- Dockyard: isolated Docker daemon with sysbox-runc as default runtime, `--iptables=false`
    46→- App containers use `runtime: runc`, only sandbox containers use sysbox
    47→- Dockerfile: `rails:1000` user inside container, NOT `sandcastle:220568`
    48→
    49→## File Locations
    50→- Sandbox image: `images/sandbox/` (Ubuntu 24.04, DinD, SSH, dev tools)
    51→- Go CLI: `vendor/sandcastle-cli/`
    52→- Installer: `installer.sh`, `install-defaults`
    53→- Services: `app/services/sandbox_manager.rb`, `tailscale_manager.rb`, `system_status.rb`
    54→
```

diff --git a/CLAUDE.md b/CLAUDE.md
index b75617f..bfbe03e 100644
--- a/CLAUDE.md
+++ b/CLAUDE.md
@@ -66,6 +66,7 @@ Both concerns provide `require_admin!` for admin-only actions.
 
 Business logic lives in plain Ruby service classes, not models or controllers:
 - `SandboxManager` — Container lifecycle (create/destroy/start/stop/snapshot/restore). Uses the `docker-api` gem to talk to the Docker daemon via socket.
+- `TerminalManager` — WeTTY web terminal sidecar lifecycle (see Web Terminal section below)
 - `TailscaleManager` — Per-user Tailscale sidecar lifecycle (see Tailscale section below)
 - `SystemStatus` — Docker daemon stats for admin dashboard
 
@@ -84,6 +85,28 @@ The sandbox image (`images/sandbox/`) is Ubuntu 24.04 with Docker-in-Docker, SSH
 - Docker daemon MTU matches container's eth0 to avoid fragmentation
 - The `entrypoint.sh` creates the user, injects SSH keys, starts dockerd, then runs sshd
 
+### Web Terminal (WeTTY)
+
+Browser-based terminal access via [WeTTY](https://github.com/butlerx/wetty) sidecar containers. Each terminal open spawns an ephemeral WeTTY container that SSH's into the sandbox.
+
+**Architecture:**
+- WeTTY container: `sc-wetty-{user}-{sandbox}` on the `sandcastle-web` Docker network
+- SSH keypair: generated per-open in `/data/wetty/{full_name}/`, private key copied into WeTTY container via `docker exec` + base64 (bind mounts don't work because Rails runs inside a container with a named Docker volume for `/data`)
+- Traefik dynamic config: `terminal-{id}.yml` in `/data/traefik/dynamic/` with forwardAuth middleware
+- WeTTY uses `COMMAND` env var with full SSH command including tmux (`tmux new-session -A -s main`)
+- `SANDCASTLE_TERMINAL_URL` env var controls the redirect target (needed when Rails is behind a different port than Traefik)
+
+**ForwardAuth flow (`TerminalController#auth`):**
+- Traefik sends each request to `http://sandcastle-web:80/terminal/auth` with `X-Forwarded-Uri`
+- Rails validates the session cookie and sandbox ownership (owner or admin)
+- Unauthenticated users get redirected to the login page with `return_to_after_authenticating` set to the terminal URL
+- Returns 200 to allow, redirect to login, or 401 for unauthorized
+
+**Important gotchas:**
+- Sandbox entrypoint must **append** SSH keys (not overwrite) because all sandboxes for the same user share `/home/{user}` via bind mount
+- `remove_pubkey` uses end-of-line regex anchor (`$`) to avoid substring collisions between sandbox names
+- Traefik config is written early in `TerminalManager#open` (before container creation) with a 1-second sleep to allow Traefik to detect the new route before the browser redirect arrives
+
 ### Tailscale System
 
 Per-user Tailscale sidecars connect sandbox containers to a user's own tailnet. Each user gets their own sidecar joining **their** tailnet (not a shared one).

     SM --> TS["TailscaleManager"]
     TS --> TSC["Tailscale\nSidecars"]
 ```
 
-- **Backend:** Rails 8.1 (Ruby 4.0), SQLite, Solid Queue/Cache/Cable
+- **Backend:** Rails 8.1 (Ruby 4.0), PostgreSQL, Solid Queue/Cache/Cable
 - **CLI:** Go + Cobra (`vendor/sandcastle-cli/`)
 - **Containers:** Docker + Sysbox (`sysbox-runc`) for sandboxes
+- **Web terminal:** [WeTTY](https://github.com/butlerx/wetty) sidecar containers with Traefik routing
 - **Auth:** Session-based (web) + API tokens (`sc_` prefix, bcrypt)
 - **Sandbox image:** Ubuntu 24.04 with Docker-in-Docker, SSH, dev tools

 See [DEPLOY.md](DEPLOY.md) for registry setup, CI/CD pipeline, and deploy workflows.

+- [Sysbox](https://github.com/nestybox/sysbox) by Nestybox — the container runtime enabling secure Docker-in-Docker sandboxes
